# Decision log

Every call made on this project, who made it, and why. **Author** = ruled
by you. **Claude** = my call on a minor ambiguity; overturn any of them
freely. Status says whether a row still holds; the design docs in `design/` describe the current rules.

| # | Date | Decision | By | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | 2026-10-04 | Finish line is a game you can open and send units through a little adventure. Polish is the goal, but we start simple. | Author | Interview | current |
| D2 | 2026-10-04 | Fresh Godot 4.7 project in `game/`, GDScript. V8's `godot/` copy is a read-only parts bin. | Author | Interview | current |
| D3 | 2026-10-04 | "Port wholesale" covers combat mechanics only: element application, tile charge, weapon skills, turn and map framework. Story, characters, look and voyage stay out. | Author | Interview | current |
| D4 | 2026-10-04 | Life is dropped. Seven elements: fire, water, ice, thunder, wind, dark, light. | Author | Interview | current |
| D5 | 2026-10-04 | Staff is added as the mage weapon. Its damage uses the spell formula (+ willpower). | Author / Claude (formula) | Interview | current |
| D6 | 2026-10-04 | Willpower, wisdom and intelligence become one stat, **willpower**. Wherever the brief says one of them, read willpower. | Author | Interview | current |
| D7 | 2026-10-04 | Stats start at 1–6, reach around 100 by the boss, and are hard-capped at 1000 for now. | Author | Interview. Reading of "up to 100 at the point" + "up to 1000 for now" | current |

| D14 | 2026-10-04 | 100 XP per level. | Author | Mid-build note | current |

| D16 | 2026-10-04 | Gear tiers E–A, each spanning two fights (E for 1–2 … A for 9–10). The stat range widens by +1 at the bottom and +3 at the top per tier. | Claude | Extends the brief's "E +0–3, D +1–6, C +2–9, etc." | current |
| D17 | 2026-10-04 | Move is 4 hexes for everyone; equipment and skills change it. Speed only sets turn order. | Claude | The brief has no move stat | superseded by D359 (move by weapon) |
| D18 | 2026-10-04 | V9 tournament docs moved to `archive/v9-tournament/`. | Claude | Not this game | current |

| D20 | 2026-10-04 | Elevation: a step can rise at most 2 levels, and each level climbed costs +1 move. Dropping down is free. Line of sight is blocked by jagged hexes and by any hex 2+ levels above both ends. Units never block sight. | Claude | The brief gives elevation in maps but no rule for it | walking cap superseded by D360, then D371 (jump 2, lance 4); forced moves keep 2 |
| D21 | 2026-10-04 | Expertise goes E→A at 10 points per rank, the same rate as affinity. | Claude | The brief gives the awards (+1/+3) but no thresholds. A is about 40 attacks in. | current |
| D22 | 2026-10-04 | Everyone starts at affinity rank 1 in their own element and expertise E in every weapon. | Claude | Gives "starting elemental affinity" a number | current |
| D23 | 2026-10-04 | Hit basis: weapon attacks and skills add 5 × expertise rank (E=0 … A=4); spells add 5 × affinity rank instead. | Claude | Reading the brief's "or" | current |
| D24 | 2026-10-04 | Affinity resistance (5%/rank same element, 2.5%/rank opposite, ice 2.5%/rank against all) adds to resist **chance**. It does not reduce damage directly. | Claude | The brief puts it in the resist-chance formula | current |
| D25 | 2026-10-04 | Only magic rolls resist: staff spells and weapon skills cast with an element. Basic weapon attacks never roll resist. | Claude | Brief: "if magic effect, roll to resist" | current |
| D26 | 2026-10-04 | Roll order is hit → glance → crit → resist. Crit is rolled only on a clean (non-glancing) hit. Hit chance is clamped to 5–100%, and a hit always does at least 1 damage. | Claude | The brief places glance and resist but not crit | current |
| D27 | 2026-10-04 | Level-up: every stat +1. The weapon's stat gets +2, and so does the matching armour stat when 2+ pieces share a weight class (heavy→def, ranger→spd, wizard→res). | Claude | Reading of the brief's "if else, level by 1" | current |
| D28 | 2026-10-04 | Pistols have range 5 (V8's flintlock had 7, but with distance falloff, which is gone). Daggers get +1 move. | Claude | The new hit formula has no range falloff | current |
| D29 | 2026-10-04 | Data CSVs are imported as `keep` (`tools/keep_csv.py`), and the self-test enforces it. | Claude | By default Godot turns every CSV into translation files | current |
| D30 | 2026-10-04 | Bark selection and trust rules from `design/BARKS.md` are adopted **provisionally**, pending your review at Gate 1. Rest healing is voiced by the downtime `rest` reaction rather than Healing Received (this narrows D10). Enemies don't bark. | Claude (bark lane) | The brief gives the events but not the rules | partly superseded by D127 (rest action and its reaction removed) |
| D31 | 2026-10-04 | Melee can't reach a target more than 2 levels above or below. | Claude | Matches the climb limit (D20) | current |

| D33 | 2026-10-04 | `design/EQUIPMENT.md` and `design/ROSTER.md` are adopted **provisionally** for Gate 1. | D34 | 2026-10-04 | HP = 100 + **2** × CON (the brief said 5). | Author | Gate 0 ruling on fight length | superseded by D137 |

| D36 | 2026-10-04 | Enemy squads are 3 roster characters not in your squad. They gain ⌊(n−1)×2/3⌋ levels, carry a weapon at the fight's tier, and from fight 3 one armour piece. | D37 | 2026-10-04 | Downtime values: train element +5 affinity, train weapon +5 expertise (half a rank each), rest +40 XP, hone +1 to a random stat line, re-enchant rerolls the item's enchantment, search gives 1 random item at the current tier, train ability +1 rank. An action with nothing to act on falls back to rest. | Claude | The brief names the actions, not the amounts | superseded by D85, D127 |
| D38 | 2026-10-04 | Re-imbue: armour enchantments move to any armour piece; weapon enchantments move only to weapons that enchantment lists. The source item is left plain. | Claude (equipment lane) | | superseded by D202 (re-imbue removed) |
| D39 | 2026-10-04 | Losing a regular fight ends the run (roguelike). Everyone is fully healed between fights. A win advances; the run autosaves at every screen change. | Claude | The brief says nothing about losing before the boss | partly superseded by D79 (a loss no longer ends the run) |
| D40 | 2026-10-04 | Equipping a weapon of another class is allowed if your expertise in that class is at least the item's tier, and it changes the unit's weapon class. | Claude | Brief: "requires the rank or higher expertise in the weapon type" | current |
| D41 | 2026-10-04 | Recruits join the squad on top of the six. The squad can grow, but you still deploy 3. | Claude | Brief: "recruit enemy from previous fight" | current |
| D42 | 2026-10-04 | Black limbs get a thin **white** contour (inverted hull); white shapes keep black contours. It disappears on white tiles and keeps figures readable against the black sky and dark halls. | Claude | Black stick figures vanished against the starfield in the first screenshots | current |
| D43 | 2026-10-04 | Music cues use your two loops: title = BlackWhite Loop; roster = the same loop at 0.85×; downtime 0.8×; combat = Drum Beat; boss = Drum Beat at 1.12×. Loops cut on the bar line (8 bars), not at the file end. Placeholder until Phase 6. | Claude | Docx: "use it for title screen, slow it down for roster picking" | partly superseded by D66 (tempos and layering) |
| D44 | 2026-10-04 | Weapon-skill interpretations from the skills lane: Energized Shot hits only its target, while Dualthrow hits everyone on its trail. Charge stops at allies and blocked hexes. Jumps (Vault, Daggerleap) take no fire-crossing damage, but Charge does. Riposte doesn't end the turn, and the guard drops at your next turn. A basic pistol shot also lays the loaded trail. No moving during a follow-up, and one follow-up per turn. | Claude (skills lane) | Full list in the lane's report and the `skills.gd` comments | current |
| D45 | 2026-10-04 | Gear effects: flat damage numbers become % HP at ×4/3 (Explosive 4%/point). Ability rank scales strength ×(1 + 0.25 × (rank − 1)), capped at rank 3. An unequipped ability type auto-equips the first one learned. An element enchantment does nothing until the wearer has learned that element. Eruptions hurt allies too. | Claude (effects lane) | `design/EQUIPMENT.md` §8.1 | current |
| D46 | 2026-10-04 | The Giant occupies 7 hexes. Any of its hexes can be targeted, and area attacks hit it once. It needs its whole footprint free to stand anywhere, reads tiles at its centre only, and spawns on the first enemy spawn where it fits. | Claude (effects lane) | Brief: "a tile and the radius around the tile" | current |
| D47 | 2026-10-04 | Base rig v1: 2.2 units tall, about 3.9 heads, 20 bones, 6 sockets, about 2.8k triangles. Joints are capped with ball geometry because Godot's skinning pinches thin tubes. Clothing copies its weights from a `fit_body` proxy. | Claude (rig lane) | `design/art/RIG.md`; awaiting your approval before hair/clothing/gear are built on it | current |
| D48 | 2026-10-04 | Esc backs out one step at a time: forecast → skill aiming → **undo the move**. Undo restores position, HP lost crossing fire, tiles laid while moving, and ability triggers. Acting or ending the turn makes the move final. | Author | Playtest request; V8 had no undo | current |
| D49 | 2026-10-04 | The combat camera is V8's rig: left-drag orbits (pitch 15–85°), a left click without dragging picks, right/middle-drag pans scaled by distance, wheel zooms ×0.9, arrows pan, Space recentres, FOV 34°, and the follow lags (FOLLOW_LAG 4). Added: Q/E rotate 60°. End turn moved from Space to **T**, since V8 used Space to recentre. | Author / Claude (key map) | "the map rotate controls … temporal sea had" | current |
| D50 | 2026-10-04 | HUD in V8's visual language (ADR-0023 weights and box metrics, `BWStyle`), translated to black and white: gold becomes white, faction colours become fills, element colour is the only hue. Turn order uses head icons, unit cards have ticked HP bars, and floating HP bars sit over units. The action menu floats beside the unit on its outer side, so the board between units stays clickable. | Claude | "UI could use polish, refer to temporal sea" | partly superseded by D60, D156 (menu placement, head icons) |
| D51 | 2026-10-04 | The window opens maximized, is resizable, and F11 / Alt+Enter toggles fullscreen. The UI stretches from the 1600×900 design size (canvas_items) and 3D renders at native resolution, so 4K works. | Author | "able to scale to 4k" | current |
| D52 | 2026-10-04 | Hair contour is a **darker value of the hair's own hue** (inverted hull, value 0.36 of the element colour), not black. Hair's inner contour (undersides, stubble, part lines, strand lines) is a second material slot `hair_shade` at value 0.62 of the same hue. One per-instance `tint` drives fill, shade and contour. `BWHair.set_contour(false)` gives a black line if a scene needs it. | Claude (hair lane) | Reference panels draw hair with a same-hue line; `design/art/HAIR.md` | partly superseded by D146, D147 (tint gradient, dark-hue contours) |
| D53 | 2026-10-04 | Weapons: one mesh per weapon, white with a black hull and a small accent tinted by element. Auras are a fresnel shell plus particles (`aura.gdshader`). Metadata lives in `art/weapons/weapons.json`. Daggers come in pairs, bows sit in the left hand, and two-handers (plus the flamberge) mark a second-hand target. | Claude (weapons lane) | `design/art/WEAPON_MODELS.md` | current |
| D54 | 2026-10-04 | Armour: **one mesh per piece**; the 7 "of {element}" variants are the accent material tinted by the enchantment's element, grey when plain. This replaces the `{item}_{element}` model naming in EQUIPMENT.md §3. Rigid pieces sit on sockets; bending pieces are skinned to the rig. Hats hide only the hair that would poke through (`hair_mode`). | Claude (equipment lane) | `design/art/EQUIPMENT_MODELS.md` | current |
| D55 | 2026-10-04 | Shader instance-uniform slots are fixed project-wide: dim = 0, tint = 1, accent = 2, anything new at 3+. | Claude | Stops Godot's "different indices" warning | current |
| D56 | 2026-10-04 | Clothing: parametric shells weighted from `fit_body`, built against a clipping check (the build fails if a limb pokes through in 6 test poses). Shading comes from a 3×3 palette texture: dark/mid/light × fill/fold/contour. The contour is white on dark garments and black on mid and light ones. Hoods are worn down. The scarf adds one spring bone, `scarf_tail`. | Claude (clothing lane) | `design/art/CLOTHING.md` | current |
| D57 | 2026-10-04 | Character assembly: armour hides what it covers. Closed chest armour trims the top to its sleeves, robes and full leg armour hide the trousers, and chaps swap trousers for tight shorts. Long hair tilts back to clear collars. Poses are static key poses solved by two-bone IK per weapon style. Blades roll to show their flat face to the camera. The stand-ins stay behind `BWUnitView.use_rig = false`. | Claude (assembly lane) | `design/art/CHARACTERS.md`; roster screen runs at 3.05 ms a frame | partly superseded by D63 (static key poses became clips) |
| D58 | 2026-10-04 | During an attack cutscene, anything between the camera and the fighters (pillars, raised tiles, bystanders) is hidden for the shot. The move-range highlight is a light wash so the board still reads white. | Claude | Seen in the Gate 3 renders | current |
| D59 | 2026-10-04 | Movement and attack radius are V8's display, translated to black and white. Each region gets a flat fill plus a fading ribbon rising only from its outer edge, and there's a ribbon path arrow with an arrowhead. The move region is a grey wash rimmed in the **acting unit's element colour**. The attack radius is ink: shown once the unit can't move, while aiming (the skill's legal targets), and for follow-ups. The splash ("this will be hit") is the skill's element colour, which replaces V8's amber. As in V8, there is no threat zone before moving and no enemy-range on hover. | Author / Claude (colour mapping) | "look up the movement radius and attack radius … apply them" | current |
| D60 | 2026-10-04 | The action menu has a fixed slot top right, the same slot the forecast uses. It no longer follows the unit. | Author | "UI stays on the right side … doesn't flutter" | current |
| D61 | 2026-10-04 | Hair shine mixes 42% toward white, down from 86%: a lighter tint of the hue, not a white patch. | Author | "a bit too white, like a bald spot" | superseded by D64 |
| D62 | 2026-10-04 | Animation style bar approved. Moves use a **run** (~0.4 s per hex) by default; the walk (0.667 s per hex) is for short or careful steps. Some foot slide is acceptable for pace. | Author | Gate 4 | current |
| D63 | 2026-10-04 | Animation clip matrix: one clip library per weapon style, every pose a clip. Moves of 2+ hexes run with an authored start and stop (1 hex walks, under 35% HP limps). The flamberge guard is now low and ready (fists at the right hip, blade out to her right, tip at chest height) because a raised blade covered her face from the side cameras; the approved idle motion is kept relative to it. **Author to confirm the new guard.** | Claude | Phase 4 matrix; `design/art/ANIMATION.md` | current |
| D64 | 2026-10-04 | Hair shine removed entirely (supersedes D61). The hair_shine slot stays but shades like the fill, so there is no glare on top of the heads. | Author | "remove the glare or light reflect from the top of their heads" | current |
| D65 | 2026-10-04 | Idles calmed: every standing clip (idle, idle_bouncy, idle_fidget, idle_look, idle_weapon, wounded; all styles) plays at 22% of its authored motion. A bake pass scales motion about the loop's own average pose (so Alexandra's approved idle keeps its shape and posture, only smaller) or, for one-shot variants, about the guard. idle_bouncy is retired as a home idle (everyone stands in the calm idle); idle rates are 0.85–1.0; variant intervals roughly doubled (8–28 s). Review: `design/art/anim_idle_calm.gif`. | Author | "the most bouncy thing I have ever seen, tone it down a ton or remove it" | partly superseded by D80 (weapon holds and action cadence) |
| D66 | 2026-10-04 | Music is layered and beat-synced (`BWMusic`): every layer of a tempo set plays sample-locked in one AudioStreamSynchronized and a cue sets layer volumes. Cue changes land on the next bar and combat intensity (0–2, from HP and KOs) on the next beat. The tempo changes are **pre-rendered sets**, pitch-preserving WSOLA: roster and rest use the slow set (102 BPM, the docx's "slow it down"), the boss uses +10% (132.3 BPM), everything else 120. A runtime `pitch_scale` + PitchShift option exists (`BWMusic.runtime_tempo`) but is off, because Godot's phase vocoder smears the bass-heavy loop. | Claude (audio lane) | `design/audio/AUDIO.md` | current |
| D67 | 2026-10-04 | Layers come from what the loops actually contain. The BlackWhite loop is 95% below 300 Hz, has nothing above 2.4 kHz, and has no separable percussion: its HPSS onsets are random against the grid. So no perc layer ships. **air** is the loop's harmonic part an octave up, and the hats come from the drum beat (`drums_top`). The Low fish takes are a male voice (speech-like, 120–200 Hz); they became `voice_pad`, an unintelligible granular pad on the loop's bass roots, used softly in rest, combat peaks and the boss. | Claude (audio lane) | Measured; numbers in `layers.json` | current |
| D68 | 2026-10-04 | Audio attaches by **listening**: `BWAudioDirector` watches the scene tree and gives unit views, combat and screens their listeners. Combat, screens and animation code have no audio calls. The price is that it reads a few private fields (`_queue`, `_views`, `_sel`, `_chosen`, `_go`, the animator's foot lock). If one is renamed, that sound goes quiet; nothing crashes. | Claude (audio lane) | Avoids edit collisions with the reactions lane | current |
| D69 | 2026-10-04 | Barks: each clip starts at its voice (the files carry 0.4–0.9 s of silence) and is levelled by a per-clip gain (the files sat 25 dB apart). Hits make the defender grunt (Oof / Ouch grunt / Oogh), and strikes sometimes shout (Hiyah / Yah). A unit can grunt once every 3.5 s, with 0.9 s between any two grunts. Reaction variants speak on their own markers: rage's `shout` → No/Hiyah, shrug's `catch` → Hmm, stumble → Oogh, flinch → Ouch grunt, knockback → Oof. | Claude (audio lane) | Mapping from the brief; "flinch"/"knockback" lines are my picks | current |
| D70 | 2026-10-04 | Mix: buses Master (limiter at −1 dB), Music −7 (ducks ~4 dB under Voice, −11 dB under stings), SFX −3, Voice −1, UI −6. Volumes are 0..1 per bus via `BWAudio.set_volume`, saved to `user://audio.cfg`. Markers that can be foreseen play up to 1.5 frames early to cancel the 3D player's physics-tick start, which measured within ±9 ms. Impacts at frame 0 can't be foreseen and land 9–18 ms late. Raising `physics/common/physics_ticks_per_second` to 120 would halve that; it's a project-wide setting, so I left it. | Claude (audio lane) | Capture analysis, `design/audio/capture_report.json` | current |
| D71 | 2026-10-04 | Hit-reaction variety: five stricken variants per weapon style (flinch, shrug, stumble, knockback, rage), each with `impact` on f0. The cutscene picks by context (BWReactionPick: damage as % of max HP, crit, glance, HP under 35%, friendliness, the tagline vibe, small/fast and heavy builds, a seeded 0.75-1.25 jitter); the table is in ANIMATION.md. Misses still dodge, glances and resists still block (a glance can also flinch or shrug), a normal melee hit can still fumble, KO blows still kneel. The knockback's half-hex travel is faked in the clip (the unit stays in its hex). The plain "hit" is per character: only cocky characters keep Alexandra's head-shake. | Author / Claude (weights, thresholds 8% / 22%) | "Increase variance in hit animations" | partly superseded by D153 (tagline vibe input removed) |
| D72 | 2026-10-04 | Projectiles modelled (arrow, bullet, bolt) in the weapon style, `game/art/weapons/projectiles/`. Arrows arc and stick in the chest for 0.7 s then shrink away; bullets fly straight at 38 u/s with a tracer and a muzzle-flash star; bolts spin inside the element aura. Bows nock an arrow from the new `nock` marker to `release`, with the string drawn to the hand (the limbs do not flex). The second dagger smears too. | Claude | Temporal Sea had only 2D blobs; brief | partly superseded by D164, D165 (bow draw, arrow flight) |
| D75 | 2026-10-04 | Roster, codex, intro and results (playtest notes). Roster header is "Select your Six Contestants"; picks fly into six portrait slots across the top (rendered once per character, faceless head + hair, element-coloured frame); empty slots are a dimmed outline head; clicking a filled slot unpicks. The stat spread panel is unchanged. **Info** (I) opens `BWCodex`, a self-contained overlay (`BWCodex.summon(host, tab)`, Esc closes) with Elements / Weapons / Stats / Terrain tabs, all read live from BWTiles, BWFormulas, BWSkills and the CSVs. The intro card is replaced by a loading screen that orbits all five maps with crossfades and the map name small, ending on the first fight's map with "Ready to fight" (any key; a key during the orbit skips to it); no mention of the Giant. Results is a centred BWStyle box, item names in their enchantment's element colour, no Continue button: any key or click after 1.2 s. | Author / Claude (layout, timings, codex wording) | Playtest notes: roster, "info" button, intro "a bit boring", Continue "super far to the left" (the screen never sized to the window; fixed for results, intro and end card) | partly superseded by D84, D121, D156 (intro, results, portraits) |
| D76 | 2026-10-04 | New weapon class **fists** (martial/STR; Jab 8, range 1, speed +2, move +1, levels STR): the daggers' counterpart, blunt and elemental. Skills: **Flurry** (adjacent, 3 strikes at 45% of power 12, each rolled; the last lays the element on the target's hex), **Uppercut** (power 12, knocks back 1 hex; +50% slam if jagged ground, a too-steep rise or a unit stops it; map edge and immune targets don't slam), **Palm Burst** (power 9, pours 2 steps into the target's hex). All cd 2, per element. Items hand_wraps (str/spd), brass_knuckles (str/dex), gauntlets (str/def), 5–6 enchantments each; three new ones (Pummeling: Flurry 4 strikes; Rebound: Uppercut +1 hex; Welling: Palm Burst +1 step) reuse `multi_hit` / `knockback` / `cast_step_plus` with a `skill=` param. Fists drop as loot and in the shop; a recruit brings a spare tier-E pair. Roster starting weapons unchanged. Models are worn on both hands, the left a mirrored mesh; poses are a static fallback until the animation lane authors clips. | Lead (design) / Claude (numbers, slam edge cases, recruit gift) | Author: "look into making fists weapons"; EQUIPMENT.md §2 Fists | current |
| D77 | 2026-10-04 | Pre-battle placement is **drag and drop**. You can drag a placed unit, or a squad row, onto a ringed deploy hex, and a dimmed ghost plus a label ("Move here", "Swap with X", "Send X in, bench Y") show where it will land. The hex under the cursor decides the result. An empty deploy hex is a plain move. A hex with a unit on it swaps the two units, and a squad row dropped there sends that unit in and benches the other. Dropping a unit on another unit's body away from any deploy hex also swaps them. A click only selects. A click on an empty deploy hex moves the selected placed unit there and never swaps. Every deploy hex has a bold ink ring. The V8 camera rig is fitted at a 60° pitch from behind your side, so all deploy rows and the enemy start sit between the panels. Left-drag on empty ground orbits the camera, and a press on a unit starts a drag instead. You can hover any unit on the map for its card, which shows stats, element, weapon and gear. | Author / Claude (swap rule reading) | "click and drag to place … it defaults to swapping"; `design/art/ui_prebattle_placement.png`, `_drag.png` | current |
| D78 | 2026-10-04 | Pre-battle gear. **Equipment** has a live paperdoll: the unit's 3D model, turnable, with Head, Chest, Legs and Main hand slot boxes, next to an inventory grid. **Item tiles** are icons rendered once from the real glbs and cached to `user://item_icons/v1/`, with a tier badge and an element-coloured border. **One item card** serves equipment and the shop. It shows the name in the element colour, stat deltas against what is worn in that slot (+ in white, − dimmed), the passive or "No passive", what the item teaches after 2 battles, and whether the unit can equip it. Clicking a tile tries it on the model, and a double-click or a drag onto a slot equips it. The **stat panel** shows the base stat large with "(+x)" for equipment (gear and passives). Bars share a squad-wide scale (at least 20, rounded up to 10s). HP, Move and Speed show their BWFormulas calc on hover; `BWFormulas.move`/`speed` were added read-only. Affinity shows as pips and expertise as letter grades. The **shop** uses the same grid and card: pick yours, pick theirs, compare, confirm. Whistling cut. | D261 | 2026-10-07 | **Ice slides (Element Overhaul, ELEMENTS-v3 §2).** Glaze is slippery (a glazed charged hex that isn't a pillar; stasis doesn't slip). A unit that ENTERS one walking, pushed or pulled slides on in its direction, free, until non-ice standable ground (it stops ON it), a drop of any height (onto the lower hex, stop, no fall damage), the map edge (stop, no slam) or `SLIDE_MAX` 6; blocked (rock, a unit, a pillar or wall, a rise of 1+) it stops before it and, if it slid 1+, **slams 8% to both**. **A slide ends the walk, then +1 move** (author ruling), once a turn. Crossing damage applies on slide hexes (frozen fire is a grill); order inside an action: hit, paint, pushes, slides, slams. `BWSlides.slide_path(board, tiles, unit, from, dir) -> {path, stop, slam_target, slam, into}` is pure and the hook for every displacement (`_displace`, Charge's shove, the obelisk pulse; wind lanes build on it). `reachable()` treats ice as enter-to-slide and lists slide END hexes (cost = the step onto the ice), so the AI scores where it ends up; a slam can't be undone. A unit's **used element** is its highest affinity (ties: its focus, then its native element): affinity is earned by using an element in battle (+1 an attack, +3 a KO) and by training it, and the run keeps no per-element damage, so it is the cleanest per-unit signal (Claude). A worn piece stays unless another beats it by 3+ stat points (no churn over 1-point rolls; Claude), then the lower uid (determinism). Cursed pieces (an enchantment or imbue row marked cursed) are never handed out; one already worn may stay on its wearer (opt-in respected). | Author (element, then rank) / Claude (class above element, the churn margin) | test_auto_equip | element term superseded by D403 (primary, then secondary); the rest current |

| D342 | 2026-10-07 | **Storm the Castle** (`BWCastleStorm`, `castle_storm.gd`). Phase 1: break the wooden gate; the throne is sealed (`hittable` empty), four wall guards, a yard guard and the Warden; 2 guards by the back doors at rounds 3, 5, 7. Phase 2 through D255's framework: a script (kind `storm`, units the gate and the Warden, one phase `throne` on `ko: 1`) started at round 1 (after `BWPhases.setup`, which resets the boss state); the gate's KO fires it; the mode's `on_ko` unseals the throne, cuts the waves not yet landed and emits `castle_breach`. Lose: the squad falls. ## Open — flagged, not blocking

- **Fight length vs. the D133 curve (after D137–D139).** Fights from 3 on
  still run 5–6 rounds median (target 6–9). HP per level barely moves it
  (measured, mixed, 48 runs: 12 × level and 15 × level change nothing,
  20 × level adds ~1 round), because squad damage outgrows it. What does
  work is **enemy-only HP**: an HP multiplier column on ENEMY_CURVE (fights
  3+) at ×1.5 gives 6–8 rounds, ×2.0 gives 7–10 mid / 6–7 late, and lets the
  stat multipliers drop to 1.35–1.6 (×2.0 with 1.4–1.6: mid 80–96%, late
  ~52%, so late fights would need ~1.3). Raising enemy levels to 1 per stage
  (instead of D99's 2/3) also lets multipliers fall to ≤1.6 but does not
  lengthen fights. Both are the author's call.

- **Fight 9 on the ravine (found with D76).** With a different loot roll the
  headless campaign lost fight 9 thirty times in a row, even at level 25: a
  kiting archer on ground the melee AI can't reach, once the squad's staff
  user falls. The sim now retries with a new seed and rotates the third slot
  (`tests/test_campaign.gd`). The map/AI pathology itself is untouched.

- **Resolved by D34:** **Fight length (Gate 0).** See `design/FORECAST-L1.txt`. A level-1 hit does
  about 11–19 against 105–130 HP, so 7–11 clean hits per knockout, and
  AI-vs-AI 3v3s run 9–18 rounds. Most tactics games land at 2–4 hits per KO.
  Mitigation (0.75 × DEF) also grows with stats while weapon damage stays
  flat, so fights get *longer* as the squad grows.

- **Resolved by D133, D138:** **The boss vs. D7.** The brief's boss has 500 HP and 50 in every stat. If the
  squad sits around 100 by fight 10, a 50-stat boss is not the intended wipe.
  The boss is tuned at Gate 2 once real growth numbers exist.
- **Level curve (measured).** The headless campaign (`tests/test_campaign.gd`)
  has the three fighters reaching levels 11–12, best stat ~34, by fight 10.
  The bench stays near level 1–2, so rotating deployments matters. The boss
  (500 HP, 50s) beat that squad, which is what the brief intends. Stats ~100
  are not reached in one run.
